# W11b2 rocket integration

Status: IN_PROGRESS. Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-13. Base: `ba046688` on
`codex/v1.8.0-w11b2-rocket-integration`. Root remains Claude. This worktree was clean
at entry; the other running workers use different worktrees. No other agent is started.

## Write scope and exclusions

The write scope is the rocket integration and its resource-escrow adapters, related
unit/GameTests, affected native rocket-schema fixtures, task-specific test scripts,
and this record. Existing W11b1 core files change only if an integration requirement
needs it; such changes will be identified separately. ADRs, the risk register, status
files, the content ledger, build scripts, workflows and other task records are excluded.
The fluid loader, linker, production admission, cargo and satellite deployment are
not implemented. No push, merge, rebase or delegation is permitted.

## Implementation and verification

This is an incomplete implementation checkpoint, not an integration-ready W11b2
delivery. Production escrow admission remains disabled. No Gate or task acceptance
is claimed.

### Implemented prerequisites

- Rocket wrapper schema 3 accepts genuine schema 1 and 2 and adds the exact
  `resource_escrow` section. Its canonical neutral encoding omits `ledger_id` only
  at epoch 0 with no markers. First binding uses the actual ledger UUID, never a
  fabricated UUID or a production ledger creation. Invalid sections preserve and
  quarantine the whole rocket wrapper. Future schema 4 and unknown wrapper fields
  are tested separately.
- The transfer journal now uses checked file replacement for explicit boundaries,
  autosave and stop-save. Acknowledgements identify the session-local mutation
  sequence, canonical root digest and individual record digests. The public
  `checkedWriteResult()` exposes COMMITTED, NOT_COMMITTED, UNKNOWN or UNACKNOWLEDGED,
  the current and acknowledged sequences, and whether that acknowledgement is current.
  An older COMMITTED attempt does not authorize a newer RAM mutation. The digest streams
  NC1-compatible sorted-key framing because W11b1's native canonical buffer has a
  262,144-byte cap, below the legal journal bound. The journal keeps its schema,
  checksums and quotas. Definite failure retains dirty authority; ambiguous outcomes
  use bounded target readback and clear acknowledgement when unknown. Failed writes
  retry at most once per 200 ticks through ordinary recovery; normal fresh mutations
  are not delayed by that failure backoff.
- Launch does not start countdown before acknowledgement. Destination publication,
  passenger movement and source removal follow their checked phase writes. Recovery
  acknowledges destination identity before publishing a rebuilt destination or
  discarding sources. Landed reservation removal is checked before disassembly.
  Failed preparation keeps captured authority instead of deleting an uncertain
  record. These changes need the required injected live/native tests before acceptance.
- Read-only escrow queries guard the existing Runtime/Manager/flight mutation
  layers, boarding, leaving, countdown cancellation and live flight dispatch. Forge
  mount, teleport, spreadplayers and dimension events are canceled for protected
  rockets. `/kill` is overridden. Live `/data` retains the escrow section and ignores
  protected mod payload changes, but does not intercept vanilla base-field changes
  or promise a command refusal message.
- Current-candidate native component, adapter, fuel and planetary rocket validators
  require entity schema 3 and an exact neutral section. Their snapshot, flight,
  transfer and transaction schemas are not bumped. Historical evidence is untouched.

### File areas and W11b1 change

The modified areas are `rocket/entity`, `rocket/flight/persistence`, `rocket/server`,
`resourceescrow`, rocket GameTests and unit tests, five native schema validators and
four Python validator fixture classes. `SuitMotionGameTests` also initializes its
rocket-seat positive fixture; a never-initialized rocket is correctly non-operational
and cannot pass the new movement interlock. Its hydrogen and post-dismount assertions
are unchanged. `RocketDestinationReadinessGameTests` has a checked isolated setup
file and uses the registered journal only for its ready authority effects. Its cold,
FULL-only, blocked-pad and ready-spawn assertions remain intact and non-vacuous.
`RocketTransferAudit` and the escrow-only
flight rule are extracted to keep `RocketTransferService` below 800 lines; it has
787 lines. The rocket entity remains an adapter; domain section validation is in
`RocketEscrowSection` and runtime queries/events are separate classes.

The only modified W11b1 core file is `EscrowCoordinator.java`: a six-line
`logicalLocked(UUID)` query covers durable and queued operations plus frozen
frontiers. This is required so the guard also covers pre-L1 preparation. W11b1's
writer, barrier, codec, migration policy, probes and task record are untouched.

### Policy at the nine original journal flush sites

| Site | Failure policy |
|---|---|
| Launch admission and landed-record replacement | No success, countdown or flight-ticket effect after failed acknowledgement. Retain captured authority. |
| Prepare exception cleanup | Do not perform the former remove-and-flush cleanup over uncertain authority. Retain the record for recovery. |
| Landed reservation release | No release classification/audit or disassembly materialization until exact removal is acknowledged. |
| Committed-record migration | Do not report migration or settle the record until acknowledgement. |
| Return/cancel cleanup | Old source authority permits restoring its stationary state before the removal; do not report cancellation success or release its reservation after failed removal. |
| Phase advancement | Acknowledge before new destination publication, passenger movement or source destruction; remove transient live classification on failure. |
| Recovery destination rebinding | Acknowledge before publishing the new destination UUID, source discard, duplicate discard or remount. |
| Recovered-source removal | Do not report recovered release after failed removal. |
| Reconnect-source settlement | Do not clear classifications or report released settlement after failed removal. |

Dirty/unacknowledged journal state blocks dependent transfer processing and resource
mutation. The entity guard covers current and last acknowledged logical identities;
unknown authority without a retained acknowledgement conservatively blocks all
identities until an exact retry. Transfer-service admission still uses a journal-wide
clean acknowledgement, so an
observed failed journal write can temporarily block unrelated rocket resource
operations too. This conservative availability effect must be reviewed alongside
the checked writer's measured cost.

## Remaining scope and known risks

### Local commits and regression corrections

- `844a1836` adds the neutral rocket schema, read-only interlocks, codec/migration
  tests and exact-schema oracle updates.
- `28d47381` adds checked journal acknowledgement and the authority-boundary guards.
- `60671d9a` retains current and last acknowledged logical identities for the entity
  resource guard, including removal failure; unknown writes without a retained
  acknowledgement remain conservative. Login recovery obtains a checked baseline
  before reconnecting. The same commit updates the two positive fixtures described
  above and adds the unknown-outcome fence regression.

The first complete GameTest run at `28d47381` had four failures out of 855 tests.
The dirty-journal guard prevented a fresh unrelated rocket's fuel initialization;
the motion test had a never-initialized rocket; the readiness test had a detached,
unacknowledged journal that could never pass the new write barrier; and restored
login recovery lacked a baseline acknowledgement. The corrections are in
`60671d9a`. No assertion, timeout, budget or log-checker expectation is weakened.
The failed log and checker report are retained separately from the final rerun.

### Incomplete integration

W11b2 is **not complete and must not be integrated as a finished slice**. The remaining
work is the ticket-free FENCED classification and loaded-proof re-evaluation (R2),
assembly/disassembly recovery fences, floor/reservation conversion and collection,
operator commands and durable settlement/tombstones, journal-guard reconciliation,
persisted relocation/proof invalidation (R6), the managed startup repair hint, the
full non-vacuous interlock matrix (R4), real no-ledger/old-world/downgrade S1, all
listed packaged S2 cuts including persistent failed committers, and both required
checked-journal benchmarks (the eight-transfer reference cycle and the maximum-snapshot
journal comparison). The separate IOWorker queue rerun is documented below; it does not
measure those journal costs. First binding is codec-tested, not yet wired to a real rocket apply
adapter. A1 serialization reloads and A0 decoded file reloads are not S1/S2 restarts.

In particular, ordinary recovery still uses its existing ticket-adding readiness
helpers and does not yet consult floors or protect credited duplicate election.
Do not run it against a real credited escrow world or enable admission. The live
load adapter cannot prevent vanilla relocation, and this checkpoint does not yet
persist `RELOCATED_ENDPOINT`. The journal's boundary reordering also needs actual
forced-stop regression; pure file tests do not establish those guarantees. The public
outcome API is present, but injected live-call-site coverage is still missing.

All v1.8 Required Gates G0–G9 remain unfulfilled here. Root must obtain independent
actual-diff review and finish the remaining W11b2 work before W11b3 or integration.

## Proposed risk and ADR text for Root

These are proposed entries for the **finished integration**, not assertions that
this checkpoint implements every protection. No ADR or risk-register file is edited.

### Risk A: D3-A protection scope and availability

A future or invalid escrow root refuses whole-world startup and preserves the raw
file. An invalid rocket or block-entity envelope quarantines that object and its
connected records until audited settlement or offline repair. Unserializable BE
roots inherit `GuardedChunkSaves`: denial affects the entire terrain chunk and,
after 256 denial coordinates saturate, all later terrain saves in that ServerLevel
until Level close, with no production online reset. Entity and other independent
stores may still save and diverge. Pending and uncertain operations retain shared
capacity (64/server, 8/rocket, 1/BE); admission refuses when exhausted. Tombstones
(64) are not collected in v1.8; exhausted tombstones refuse abandonment and floor
settlement. A classified fenced transfer blocks only overlapping moving station
regions; this claim requires R2 to be complete. Fenced transactions lock resources
and disassembly. Failed checked journal writes retain authority and prevent dependent
effects; journal-wide acknowledgement can temporarily block unrelated rocket resource
operations. Logs have one coordinator state/backoff line per transition, but retries,
transitions and inherited EventBus/ChunkMap/EntityStorage/IOWorker/force errors have
no lifetime cap. Protected `/data` edits are ignored; vanilla base relocation and
raw/third-party replacement are detection-only and require proof invalidation and
locked repair. Relocation, credited assembly fences, veto stalls and unsupported
floor conflicts require the whole-world offline procedure. Stop the server, back
up all Levels' terrain/entities/POI, journals/ledger/global data, playerdata and
metadata together with hashes, repair only a copy while retaining raw originals,
validate identity/prefix/conservation and run two restart cycles plus a new barrier
before replacing the complete world while stopped. Restoring single data files,
including migration backups, is unsafe. Restart alone is not repair. This entry
registers mechanics and accepts neither R-021 nor another save-veto mechanism.

### Risk B: unobservable storage failures after confirmation

S2 covers stopped-process ordering with successful forces, not power loss, OS crash,
per-file rollback, or environmental storage faults the mod cannot observe. W11b1 E1
requires a retained own-store failure to block proof and L2 even if a later force
succeeds. W11b1 E3 distinguishes such observed failures from an unrelated region-cache
eviction-close failure for which no store future reports an error. Any later write
that corrupts or replaces a confirmed record remains a residual throughout the
post-confirmation window, not only after L2. Direct IOWorker, RegionFileStorage or
ChunkStorage writes bypass Save-event/entity-decorator monitoring. After retirement,
vanilla may regenerate or omit unreadable chunk contents, causing loss with no
remaining escrow diagnosis. Consistent whole-world backups mitigate this residual.
Diagnostic reproduction is not a guarantee PASS or owner acceptance.
On Windows, portable directory forcing is unavailable; the checked writer forces
the staged file before atomic replacement. S2 does not establish power-loss durability.

### ADR-065 implementation note: W11b2 D3-A rocket integration

The integration adds checked stage/force/readback/atomic replacement for every
transfer-journal write on the server thread. The non-throwing boundary exposes exact
snapshot acknowledgement; failed or unknown writes retain authority and dirty state,
and callers may not publish success, advance dependent authority effects, clear
reservations or destroy protected objects before acknowledgement. Launch, phase,
cleanup, migration, replacement and reconnect policies are those in the nine-site
table. An absent journal baseline is checked on first need; blocked roots are never
rewritten. Rocket schema 3 has a neutral optional-ledger section only at epoch 0
without markers and binds to the actual ledger at the first acknowledged operation.
Recovery consults records, markers, floors and tombstones before readiness or effects;
FENCED classification is preserved during bounded loaded-only re-evaluation with no
ticket-adding helper. ADR-044's classified-record region rules remain unchanged.
The finished settlement protocol requires durable intent, every survivor/absence
checkpoint, checked retirement/tombstones and exact acknowledgement before unlock.
Protection scope, duration, log volume and recovery are those in Risk A; unobservable
writer residuals are those in Risk B. This note changes no accepted decision, grants
no risk acceptance and does not replace native cuts or independent implementation
review. Root must not insert unfinished mechanisms as delivered capability.

### Local implementation commits

- `bd1103c6` adds ledger-first transfer fence classification, bounded loaded-only

  remains present throughout an observation and an unavailable Level or unloaded
  end cannot promote it. Ordinary ticketing readiness excludes fenced records.
  Transfer diagnostics and station motion include fences; the moving-phase rules
  still apply only to overlapping regions. Inspection now uses visible entities
  without loading origins. Assembly credit and pending disassembly recovery are
  fenced before removal/materialization. Credited, otherwise idle disassembly is
  still permitted by the pure recovery policy. Two new unit classes and an A1
  loaded-index GameTest cover these adapters.
- `c715fe22` reserves the union of named-transfer floor keys before queued L1
  admission, including maximum section growth. Durable operations reconstruct
  reservations after a reload; NONE guards reserve and install nothing. The L2
  snapshot installs/coalesces the latest jointly proved endpoint floor in the
  same checked ledger replacement as retirement. Existing and queued references
  prevent collection. Startup and subsequent ticks collect only after a current,
  checked journal acknowledgement lacks the transfer; only the ledger callback
  publishes the removal. The permission-2 `/arce escrow status` command reports
  operation, used/reserved floor and tombstone counts without creating a ledger.
  `EscrowFloorsTest` covers capacity, restart, conversion, collection and writer
  failure/recovery using real checked files.
- `423dfaff` pins the loaded-only region query at 16 chunk columns, rejecting
  wider regions before inspecting them. `48942f04` invalidates cached recovery
  observations on Forge entity tracking loss as well as joins. The mapped Forge
  jar's `ServerLevel.EntityCallbacks.onTrackingEnd` was inspected: it posts
  `EntityLeaveLevelEvent`. `2d2fa107` keeps that bounded iteration safe even at
  integer edges and adds the edge regression.

### Remaining work, risks and W11b3 boundary

This continuation supersedes the earlier statement that transfer recovery has no
floor consultation or ticket-free fence mechanism. It does **not** close the
warning that credited-world recovery is unsafe. Production admission remains
disabled and W11b3 must not proceed to admission. Specifically:

1. The actual rocket apply/registration adapter, first real binding and journal
   guard reconciliation are still missing. The ordinary escrow scheduler still
   has no production rocket reconciliation implementation.
2. Only `status` is implemented among the operator commands. Durable connected
   settlement intent, every survivor/absence barrier, O3 tombstones and O4 unlock,
   including `settle-floor` and O-veto, remain unimplemented. The schema currently
   decodes only propulsion-fill operations, not floor-retirement settlement.
3. Command teleport/travel/mount cancellations remain in place, but the `/data`
   base-position bypass still lacks persisted RELOCATED_ENDPOINT and complete
   proof invalidation across saves and two native restarts. Live protected fields
   are ignored; vanilla command success is not a refusal oracle.
4. The complete non-vacuous R4 matrix and pending assembly/disassembly native
   recovery cases remain outstanding. The new A1 case observes fence/readiness
   adapters and visible indices; it is not a packaged operator-command or
   whole-service restart test.
5. The existing native harness still lists K/C/E and G3 cases, not W11b2's
   R1-R3, O1-O4/O-veto or G1/G2 rocket integration cuts. It uses the W11b1 test
   endpoints rather than a real rocket apply adapter. Genuine no-ledger and old
   world upgrade, pinned old-jar downgrade preservation, persistent failed
   journal committers, collection cuts and repeated integrated K/C cuts must be

### Design choices and actual coverage

The audit uses only already-loaded entity queries and the bounded ledger (64
operations). It compares endpoint identity, logical identity, type, Level, chunk,
flight origin, actual position and occupancy. It never adopts a moved position.
Live `Entity.load` keeps the protected mod wrapper while vanilla applies `Pos`;
the resulting mismatch invalidates both entity-chunk proof generations and queues
a durable RELOCATED_ENDPOINT repair. Existing manual reasons and settling phases
are sticky. Ordinary L2 cannot remove these records even with otherwise valid
proofs. No resource credit is applied by the audit.

The checked-journal audit compares the named transfer's exact digest, not the
session-local sequence. A NONE guard checks absence for its logical rocket;
unrelated journal mutations do not invalidate that absence. A failed checked
write remains TRANSFER_JOURNAL_UNPROVED; a conflicting named guard becomes manual
JOURNAL_GUARD_MISMATCH. These observations are not endpoint readback proofs.

The three native cases use a real rocket, the existing gated test source and the
existing process/Anvil inspection harness. RA_BEFORE_REPAIR stops after the moved
entity is forced but before the repair-ledger replacement: the cut has one
PREPARED/NONE record, zero rocket fuel and position x=66.5. RA_AFTER_REPAIR stops
after the REPAIR/RELOCATED_ENDPOINT generation is acknowledged. Both restart twice
with the record retained, zero fuel, unchanged protected wrapper and the rocket
still locked. The native probe first proves neutral `/tp` and forced mounting
succeed, then pending `/tp` and forced mounting refuse. Dimension travel is an
actual Forge event-cancellation test, not physical inter-Level travel. The live
data test calls the same `Entity.load` boundary as `/data`, not the console command.

RA_SCHEMA_TWO converts a scalar neutral fixture's wrapper to schema 2, checks the
exact migration in memory, forces its schema-3 save, hard-stops and restarts twice
with no escrow ledger. This is a native schema-2 fixture, not a captured v1.7 world
or a v1.7-jar downgrade test. Relocation cases are pending, uncredited fixtures;
they do not certify replay or retirement of a real credited rocket.

### Journal cost results and limits

`journal-benchmark.json` is MEASURED_WITH_REFERENCE_LOAD_GAPS, not PASS. The final
8-transfer, 2,048-block-snapshot cycle makes 48 acknowledged checked writes across
admission, four authority phases and retirement. Owner-write interval mean is
86.35 ms, p50 90.50 ms, p95 113.80 ms, p99/maximum 122.28 ms. Mutations before
`saveChecked` are outside these intervals; encoding, digest, stage, force,
readback and replacement are inside. These costs already show substantial
synchronous server-thread pauses; no performance budget was relaxed.

The maximum fixture has 64 disjoint records, 2,048 blocks per snapshot, source
snapshot 1,048,565 bytes, destination exactly 1,048,576 bytes, and journal
134,368,966 bytes under the unchanged 146,866,176-byte limit. Current vanilla
in-place serialization/write takes 4,061.92 ms; checked serialization/write takes
5,266.72 ms and acknowledges the snapshot. Sampled peak Java heap is 1,498,536,448
bytes under the 4-GiB cap. The comparator is the current vanilla algorithm, not a
pinned old binary. Payload padding is synthetic legal codec data, not a real
reference-world fixture. No reference-node load, player load, native watchdog,
RSS, soak or integrated barrier/chunk-load-latency benchmark was run. Thus the
full benchmark acceptance remains open despite these two measured diagnostics.

The writer captures immutable domain references on the server thread. Encoding,
digesting, staging, force, readback and replacement belong to its single IO
thread. It retains one active generation and one successor, publishes exact
acknowledgements on the owner thread, and retains unresolved generations for
bounded-cadence retries. Gameplay continuations wait for their exact committed
generation and a clean current acknowledgement containing the same per-rocket
authority. Shutdown drains authority without executing gameplay callbacks;
crash teardown joins without publishing effects. Production escrow admission
remains disabled. Final commands, evidence and remaining items follow after
verification.

The implementation is locally committed as `a86d9a20`. Autosave and explicit
flush share the writer; `flushChecked` requests IO without joining. A launch
returns JOURNAL_PENDING with the source unchanged, and the player request ledger
reports success only from the acknowledged continuation. Countdown and transit
clocks start when their gameplay transitions actually run, not while IO waits.
Destination spawn, passenger movement, source removal, phase advancement,
destination rebinding, migration classification and reservation release wait.
Rollback/source restoration retains the existing acknowledged source authority;
removal, unlock, pad release and success wait for its removal acknowledgement.
An arrival disassembly attempt returns busy while its reservation removal is
pending; retry after acknowledgement is required, and fuel-disposal consent is
revalidated rather than consumed by an unacknowledged success.

The GameTest presence/resource fixtures explicitly drain only on a GameTestServer
to serialize deliberately injected cuts. This is not production or native
evidence of a synchronous gameplay barrier. Ordinary flight tests assert
JOURNAL_PENDING and unchanged source state, then await their original flight,
conservation and uniqueness assertions without larger deadlines. New writer
tests cover owner-thread publication, exact generations, disjoint successors,
failure/unknown retention, two-request/two-effect bounds, stop and abort; the
recovery completion cache is bounded at 64 and tested at the limit and beyond.
The codec extraction keeps SavedData below 500 lines and the transfer service at
795, below the TASK's 800-line ceiling. No save format or limit changes.

### Remaining scope and protection-risk note for Root

Credited-rocket recovery remains unsafe for the exact reasons already identified:
`RocketEscrowAudit.attempt` checks the journal guard and loaded placement but
never registers actual endpoint prefix state, applies an exact BEFORE state,
proves both endpoint stores jointly, acknowledges L2 or prunes markers. The
default runtime driver is this audit, not a real-rocket reconciliation adapter.
Its WAITING_UNLOADED fallback can describe a loaded, otherwise valid endpoint;
it is not evidence of actual unloading. Missing identity and orphan election are
also unresolved. The pending-only native cuts cannot substitute for a credited
operation or prove its conservation. Releasing such credit would be unsafe.

Durable accept-current, abandon, settle-floor and returning-identity tombstones
are not implemented. `EscrowCommands` remains status-only. There is no frozen
connected-component observation/intent, confirmation digest, O2 durable missing
endpoint absence proof, O1-O4 acknowledged settlement protocol, net-resource
audit or returning-identity freeze path. Phase enum names and codec support do
not provide those mechanisms. No record-deletion shortcut was added. This is
unfinished TASK work, not a completed conservative settlement implementation.

The full final-source native matrix remains open: real-rocket integrated K0-K10
and C1/C2, R1-R3, O1-O4 and O-veto for all settlement choices/chains, G1/G2 with
actual old-jar preservation and both first-schema-save cuts, floor collection,
and persistent failed checked committers for launch and authority advancement
across two restarts. This continuation adds failure/unknown writer unit coverage,
not that native integration evidence. Physical travel and console ride/data
coverage also retain their previously recorded gaps. No complete `clean build`
was run here; release CI, reference performance, V1/V2, dedicated full-matrix,
license and other v1.8 Required Gates are not claimed.

Proposed ADR-065 implementation-note update for Root: the historical synchronous
checked-write description is superseded for ROCKET_TRANSFERS by one owner-bound
writer per journal, with immutable generation snapshots, one active request and
one successor, exact owner-thread acknowledgement and at most two dependent
continuations. Definite/unknown failures retain dirty authority and block related
effects; retries use 200 ticks. Normal shutdown freezes gameplay continuations,
drains writes and joins before storage release; crash teardown joins without
publishing effects. The drain stops retrying after three completed unresolved
attempts, but joining a hung OS IO call can still delay shutdown. This note does
not change W11b1's ledger guarantees or authorize production admission.

Proposed protection-risk update: the affected journal spans at most 64 transfers
in the overworld; related logical identities remain fenced until an exact current
acknowledgement or coherent offline repair. It does not reject unrelated Level
saves. One journal error is logged per failure streak, and a failed continuation
logs its bounded generation rather than claiming success. Preserve the old/current
journal and coherent world/ledger backup for recovery; do not delete unresolved
authority. Existing W11b1 E1/E3 and later vanilla IO failures the mod cannot
observe, intermittent native BARRIER_UNSTABLE and force-field startup risks,
Windows directory-force limits, incomplete credited/settlement protocols and
the retained generated-copy cleanup remain risks. Release-test launch entry points
now return JOURNAL_PENDING; historical harnesses must wait for the acknowledged
checkpoint rather than treating initial PENDING as success.

Root owns the ADR/risk entries and independent actual-diff review. The next work
is still W11b2: actual endpoint reconciliation, durable settlement/tombstones and
the remaining native/reference-performance matrix. Only after those prerequisites
are reviewed should W11b3 admission work proceed. **Not all Required Gates or
TASK items are satisfied; no version or Gate is marked passed.**

### Candidate validation and native fault fixtures

Candidate `put` validation is now timed separately and included in the
eight-transfer benchmark's combined owner execution time. The unchanged mean,
p95 and p99 budgets are 25, 50 and 100 ms. Maximum-snapshot candidate admission
is also timed. Immutable fixture creation remains excluded and this isolated

scope and evidence pins are recorded below.

The native journal-failure fixture uses the existing console assembly, refuel
and launch adapters on a real five-block legacy-fuel rocket. An explicit release
property selects ADMISSION or ADVANCEMENT failure, and the separate hooks flag
plus empty dedicated-server check is required. Normal production and detached
test writers retain the atomic committer. The injection refuses replacement,
not validation, staging, force or decoded readback. ADVANCEMENT permits PREPARED
but also refuses its removal, so restart recovery must retain the old journal
instead of escaping the fault by deleting it. Each JVM must encounter three
failed commit attempts, save the entity and retain one source with 1,000 fuel
and no destination. After the first hard stop, two restarts repeat the failure;
source identity, owner, logical identity, snapshot/cargo, fuel and neutral escrow
section stay exact. Safe rollback from TRANSIT to FUELED is permitted by old
source authority, but the old PREPARED journal must remain exact. This fixture
does not credit an escrow operation or establish operator settlement.

### Owner-cost result and design choice

The eight-transfer, 48-write, 2,048-block-snapshot diagnostic measures candidate
`put`/`remove` validation plus capture/publication. Combined owner time is mean
4.1764 ms, p95 7.2765 ms and p99 47.3868 ms, against unchanged 25/50/100 ms
limits. Candidate validation alone is mean 3.7308 ms, p95 6.3860 ms and p99
39.3096 ms. It therefore remains validated on the owner thread; this run gives
no evidence requiring migration to the writer or removal of validation.

The maximum fixture still uses 64 transfers and exact legal maximum destination
snapshots: 1,048,576 bytes each, 134,368,966-byte journal, unchanged
146,866,176-byte journal limit. Maximum-fixture candidate admissions are mean
4.9395 ms, p95 6.9777 ms and p99 7.7518 ms; checked capture/publication is
8.5867 ms, writer work 5,576.4725 ms, sampled peak heap 1,585,020,464 bytes.
These isolated intervals exclude immutable fixture construction and complete
gameplay ticks. They are not loaded reference-server MSPT, player-load, RSS,
watchdog or soak evidence. The in-place comparison uses the current vanilla
NBT algorithm, not an executed pinned historical binary. Reference performance
acceptance remains open.

### Exact unfinished integration and safety boundary

**Credited-world recovery remains unsafe and is not implemented.** The normal
server-start scheduler selects `RocketEscrowAudit.attempt`, which checks the
acknowledged transfer journal and loaded rocket placement, then always returns
`proved=false`; an otherwise valid observation becomes WAITING_UNLOADED. It
does not register actual source block-entity and rocket frontiers, classify the
whole connected prefix as BEFORE/AFTER, apply a BEFORE debit/credit with exact
epoch/marker updates, make missing/orphan identity elections, acquire joint
native endpoint proofs, publish L2, or checkpoint marker pruning. `RocketEntity`
exposes the retained section but has no implemented live credit application
adapter. W11b1's test-endpoint reconciliation is not that production adapter.
Disabled admission prevents new production operations; it does not repair an
already-credited world. Matching totals, a placement observation or a journal
acknowledgement alone cannot prove the missing endpoint history.

**Durable operator settlement and tombstones remain unimplemented.**
`EscrowCommands` registers only `status`. There is no implemented preview and
confirm digest, actor audit and bounded frozen connected-component O1 intent,
O2 survivor/absence checkpoint, O3 acknowledged tombstone and matching journal
removal, or O4 acknowledged release/floor retirement. Generic tombstone codec
and read-side locks do not produce that protocol. No `accept-current`, `abandon`
or `settle-floor` command is added, and no successful settlement or its forced
cuts are claimed. Deleting records or equating an absent loaded entity with
durable absence would release an unproved credit and permit replay when an
identity returns; that shortcut is deliberately not implemented.

The remaining native matrix is R1-R3; O1-O4 and O-veto for accept, abandon,
settle-floor and connected chains; complete G1 with genuine old-world/old-jar
preservation and both first-schema-save cuts; full G2 physical and console
interlocks; floor collection/CF1; repeated integrated K0-K10 and C1/C2; and
unknown/later-site failed committers. The RA_SCHEMA_TWO scalar fixture is not
an actual v1.7.0 preservation run, and the pending neutral relocation fixtures
are not credited reconciliation. Existing W11b1 armor-stand cuts are not
substituted for these real-rocket integration requirements. There is no new
evidence that closes the previously recorded physical-travel, live `/data`,
full movement matrix or reference-performance gaps.

### Verified implementation and design choices

`b1d19c0d` corrects the live bay fixtures to use a native ABSTRACT propulsion
descriptor, matching schema-2 snapshots and native fuel vectors. It keeps every
existing assertion, the 80-wait bound and the 200-tick timeout. No production
resource guard is relaxed. The three fixtures exercise actual flight tickets,
checked journal callbacks and entities: a held operator attempt gets no ticket;
READY_SOURCE continues at TRANSIT altitude and publishes the 500-unit destination;
the unconsumed handoff restores the 1,000-unit stationary source; and a destination
callback captured before a hold cannot publish its entity.

The dispatch applies one-shot handoffs only during classification, including
join/leave observations. A disappearing claim without an explicit handoff stays
held. HELD drops fence reasons, retries and approvals without a fence log, excludes
ordinary ticketing and preserves unrelated station availability. A delegated
flight requires a fresh action token; it never turns the outer idle predicate
true. Escrow conflicts become a held REPAIR rather than a transfer fence. Journal
remove, rebind and phase callbacks recheck the hook after acknowledgement. The
completion cache also refuses success while its removal is unacknowledged.
`RocketTransferService` remains 796 physical lines.

The authority counts operation UUIDs, not copies or separate writer totals. Its
propulsion index includes durable records, queued admissions and the exact
captured candidate, including UNKNOWN. Successor admissions stay in the queue
until their candidate is captured. All unresolved phases count.
Admission preflights floors before reserving, and shared refusal changes neither
the issued sequence nor the ledger. There is no timeout or operation-ID release.
Producer indexes must retain references until exact durable resolution; a queued
reservation conservatively remains charged if its producer never publishes it.
The startup over-capacity test retains 65 indexed operations and refuses only new
work. No ADR decision is changed and no new save-refusal mechanism is introduced.

Files changed by area are the bay gate, dispatch and diagnostics; transfer service,
recovery, fences and escrow predicates; the runtime/manager/flight registration
bridge; `EscrowAdmissionAuthority`, coordinator and captured-writer index;

flight-ticket inspection helper only gains package visibility. There are 23
changed files relative to the authorized merge, including this record. No upstream

more frequently, with bounded waits and 30-second narration. Both executed
batches held their own lock for build, data, GameTest and native servers. C: and D:

Using a modern native fixture verifies the bay contract; it does not prove legacy
scalar-world flight/mutation compatibility. No such production fix or genuine
historical-world acceptance is claimed here. The part-1 fix is tracked in
`records/W11B2-P1F.md`; its decoded scalar-fuel regression evidence supersedes
this compatibility limitation, not the historical-world acceptance gap.

### Exact consumer handoff and unfinished integration

The shared authority is implemented only for the currently bound escrow core.
It is presently coordinator-owned and lazily bound with RESOURCE_ESCROW. W27c1
must make that same authority available for the whole server lifetime even when
there is no ledger, without manufacturing an empty ledger for a bay. It must
register BAY as NOT_LOADED before any admission, then populate the validated
startup index with every durable, queued/re-arm, active/successor and UNKNOWN
reference, including REPAIR and released records. It must implement the separate
4,096/server, 128/owner and 1 KiB lifecycle retained union, exact REJECTED pending
release and same-operation re-arm. Those receipt consumers are not implemented.
W11b3 must use the same authority after its writer-availability, endpoint, chaining
and floor preflights, rather than creating another 64/8 counter. Production
admission remains disabled.

W27d must supply the real coordinator, exact physical/action tokens and lower
entity/mount/movement scopes, invalidate proofs on both endpoint notifications,
persist and log claim transitions, and classify in the same D1 reservation call.
The ascent-complete reclassification and internal registration seam are present;
there is no real D0-D7 deployment behind the default gate in this task.

Credited-world reconciliation remains unsafe for the reasons rechecked in the
preceding continuation: the production scheduler invokes `RocketEscrowAudit`
without an actual connected-prefix source/rocket adapter and never produces a
proved result. It lacks BEFORE/AFTER application with exact epochs and markers,
missing/duplicate/orphan elections, joint durable endpoint proofs, acknowledged
L2 and marker pruning. A placement or journal observation cannot substitute for
those proofs. This continuation records that unsafe boundary rather than claiming
to repair existing credited worlds.

Durable operator settlement and tombstones are still **unimplemented W11b2 work**,

## Continuation 06

### Settlement design, written before code

An operator decision is distinct from ordinary reconciliation. A bounded intent
freezes the entire identity-connected component, the existing record digests,
actor identity, selected choice, preview digest, observation ticks and every
survivor's ledger/epoch/resource signature/markers/quantity. Missing observations
are loaded, ticking, identity-checked observations, not durability proofs. A
confirmation must match the preview, and missing observations must be repeated
at least 200 ticks later. Unloaded, duplicate, relocated, malformed or unsupported
endpoints refuse intact. Canonical hashing includes the selected choice, actor,
component, journal guards and observations. Oversized intents and tombstone
reservations refuse before any ledger mutation; there is no truncation.

O1 changes the connected records to a settling phase in one checked generation.
The optional intent is part of the operation envelope; a separate explicit kind
can represent a transfer-floor retirement without pretending it is a fluid fill.
Loaded settling records without a frozen intent remain locked legacy development
records; they cannot be treated as decisions. Unknown writes retain the exact
candidate. The matching acknowledgement, not a RAM phase, authorizes O2.

O2 must use the existing retained own-store, original-worker force and stable
readback barrier for every survivor and missing referenced slot. All proofs must
remain jointly valid through replacement. A missing observation alone cannot
authorize tombstones, journal removal or release. A veto, changed survivor,
superseding save or lost proof retains the complete decision and all locks.
After a restart every O2 proof is reacquired; no serialized boolean is proof.

O3 durably installs bounded returning-identity tombstones before any matching
journal removal. Named removals must match the frozen journal record digest and
wait for the exact checked snapshot acknowledgement without that transfer.
O4 retires records/floors only after those prerequisites, then prunes retired
markers without reducing resource epochs. No resource is reconstructed or
refunded. Actor, quantities, hashes and signed accounting remain auditable.
Floor settlement is restricted to missing protected identities and the frozen
transfer/floor, not a general floor-conflict override. Tombstones are never
collected online.

Implementation and evidence below distinguish supported boundaries from
unfinished adapters. An unimplemented O2/O3/O4 adapter must refuse, never remove
records or report settlement success. The ordinary scheduler must likewise keep
`proved=false` until connected-prefix application, identity elections, joint
proofs, L2 and pruning actually exist. Native cuts require final committed source
and JAR pins, a stop on each side of each implemented acknowledgement and two
restarts. The complete matrix, real historical-world/JAR G1, reference load and
Root's independent review remain separate acceptance requirements.

### Implemented boundary and local commit

Local commit `487ca866719dcdcb2553987feb25e2f2f1831d6b`
(`feat(escrow): persist audited connected O1 settlement intents`) implements an
O1 foundation, not completed operator settlement. Its 21 files cover the intent
model, bounded observation and preview service, optional operation envelope,
checked coordinator publication, operator diagnostics, runtime lifecycle, native
probe/runner and tests. The one-page design above is included in that commit.
No commit through `3c47fa07` is rewritten or amended.

`EscrowSettlementIntent` freezes the identity-connected records, their original
digests/phases/reasons, actor UUID/name, choice, preview/confirmation ticks,
endpoint quantities and ledger/epoch/signature/markers. Decode rejects split
components, changed records and foreign ledgers. Confirm rejects another actor,
changed digest or observations. Missing endpoints need two matching observations
at least 200 ticks apart; those observations are explicitly not durable absence.
The cache permits 64 actors, replaces an actor's own preview and expires it after
1,200 ticks. Actor names are bounded to 128 UTF-8 bytes. Existing root, operation,
reference, component and per-rocket bounds are not enlarged. Capacity reserves
one tombstone slot and up to 2,048 bytes per member before O1 publication.

`EscrowOperation`, `EscrowCodec`, `EscrowLedger`, `EscrowPrefix` and `EscrowFloors`
now represent a separate `TRANSFER_FLOOR_RETIRE` intent with the actual frozen
floor as its payload. It is not a fictitious fluid fill or resource-epoch
transition. A floor must remain present while its intent is pending. The model
and checked publication are unit-tested, but a production floor observer,
preview/confirm adapter and `settle-floor` command do not exist.

`EscrowCoordinator.beginSettlement` requires an idle, available checked writer,
no queued admission or pending transition, and the existing start spacing.
Unknown results retain the exact candidate and do not invoke a success callback.
Matching owner-thread acknowledgement publishes the intent, but does not release
locks, remove journals, create tombstones or retire floors. Lock queries include
captured writer operations, including a pending floor intent before publication.
Ordinary L2 and the reconciliation scheduler cannot retire settling records.
Earlier development settling phases without an intent stay locked; no decision
is inferred from them.

`EscrowSettlementObserver`, `EscrowSettlementOperator` and `EscrowCommands` add
permission-2 `accept-current` and `abandon` preview/confirm entry points. All
observations use already FULL, entity-loaded, ticking slots without adding
tickets or loading chunks. Real rockets are checked for identity, placement,
occupancy, stationary state and resource signatures. Entity observation is
bounded to 1,024 examined entities per slot, and relocation lookup to 64 loaded
Levels. Source observation supports only the existing explicitly enabled test
fixture. No production fluid-source adapter is registered, so ordinary worlds
refuse intact. Command output distinguishes queued from acknowledged decisions
and explicitly says checkpointing/release are unavailable. The server-lifetime
cache is cleared at shutdown/crash stop.

`RocketEscrowAudit` now reports `RECONCILIATION_UNIMPLEMENTED`, with `proved=false`,
when its loaded audit checks pass but reconciliation is absent. The scheduler
retries that transient condition every 20 ticks and cannot clear stalled
authority through it. This replaces the misleading loaded-success
`WAITING_UNLOADED` result; it is not a connected-prefix implementation.

### Settlement implementation detail, recorded before code

The authorized shared-branch merge imports `33d06319`, including W36, W33 and W38,
without conflicts. It does not rewrite any earlier commit or modify another tree.

The continuation-06 ordering is retained. O2 uses a joint own-store/force/stable
readback per physical slot, including absent slots. Every frozen survivor and
absence gets a retained token. All tokens are required together at O3 replacement
and O4 replacement; after restart they are reacquired, never deserialized as proof.
A failed/vetoed/changed/unloaded endpoint retains the complete O1 decision. A
serialized absence observation is not authorization. Processing is bounded and
uses loaded-only queries; no settlement ticket is added.

O3 installs one permanent, bounded audit tombstone per member while retaining O1
records and floors. A new independent tombstone schema 2 distinguishes the frozen
chosen physical survivors from missing identities and records the decision/actor,
endpoint quantities and resource digests. Schema 1 remains accepted with its
existing conservative lock. Schema 2 adds an acknowledged release flag: before
O4 all named identities stay locked; afterward only the chosen physical survivor
may operate, while missing and other returning physical identities remain locked.
The original named transfer remains barred from replay. Tombstones are not collected.
This detail is needed because applying the old blanket logical-ID tombstone lock
to accept-current would permanently lock even its checkpointed survivor.

After O3 acknowledgement, matching named journal records are removed only when
their checked record digest equals the frozen guard. The exact acknowledged
snapshot without those records is required; RAM absence never qualifies. A
mutation-sequence lease invalidates O4 if journal authority changes during ledger
replacement. O4 atomically removes the connected O1 records and their floors and
marks their tombstones released. Only its exact acknowledgement authorizes marker
pruning, without decreasing epochs or changing resources. Released tombstones
also provide restart-safe pruning authority if a stop precedes the callback.
Floor settlement requires both already-loaded journal ends and the missing
protected identity; it does not permit other floor conflicts to be overridden.

Native cuts must cover each endpoint acknowledgement, O3 replacement/callback,
matching journal replacement/callback and O4 replacement/callback, plus vetoes
for accept-current, abandon, floor retirement and connected decisions. Each cut
is pinned to committed executable source and the built JAR and restarted twice.
The evidence and any unsupported boundary will be reported explicitly below.

### Post-implementation review detail, recorded before the correction

The first validation build passed all 3,059 unit tests and all 972 GameTests,
including the log checker. Its native pilot passed three cuts, then failed the
floor restart's assertion that both absence slots remain concrete records.
Vanilla's later empty-entity save removes an empty slot after O4. The native
floor fixture will contain one unrelated, persistent entity in each checked
slot, so the unchanged assertion can examine durable absence records after
ordinary shutdown. This does not change the production store or release policy.
The failed pilot is preserved under `W11b2-C07/verification-01`.

Review also found that choosing a physical UUID alone does not reject a stale
copy of that same UUID. Released receipts will retain a minimum resource epoch;
at that epoch, the chosen bound state, resource signature and quantity must
match. A lower epoch, an unbound reset or different resources at the same epoch
remain locked. Recovery and transaction election will reject a candidate below
that receipt's epoch. Approved native resource changes on an already-bound,
unmarked rocket advance its epoch, without binding ordinary neutral rockets or
altering the O4 pruning operation. Tests will cover the stale same-UUID case and
the permitted monotonic mutation. Production admission and reconciliation are
still excluded.

The second validation build passed 3,061 unit tests, 972 GameTests and the log
checker. Nine accept-current cuts passed, but its first O-veto disk assertion
failed. The fixture installed the source save refusal only after O1, leaving an
interval after the debit in which vanilla could save it. The refusal will be
installed before the debit; the 5,000 mB disk assertion is retained. Failure
diagnostics will include observed source quantities rather than just a verdict.

`W42-rerun` with an 11:24 timestamp, although this runner acquired the directory
at 11:17. Its finalizer did not delete the different owner's lock. Exclusive

and every native JVM start will check the acquired owner token and stop if it
changes. No foreign lock, process or worktree is changed.

The third pinned run passed all 55 cuts with two restarts each. Subsequent policy
review found an empty-election edge case: a released receipt whose guard is
`NONE` still needs to classify a later returned journal as ledger-known before
readiness queries, and an election without the chosen survivor must not rebuild
from its historical snapshot. The receipt-history classification and the empty
transfer/transaction election will stay fenced. A loaded chosen destination may
still be kept, and normal resource mutation remains permitted. This is a
conservative receipt rule, not credited-world reconciliation. Unit and native
checks will cover it before final delivery.

The prescribed plain generated-world removal was rejected by the execution tool
at 13:13. No alternative deletion is attempted. Further verification will use a
fresh disposable GameTest working directory, with no change to assertions,
budgets, test selection or repository build scripts. A normal incremental build,

historical elections stay fenced even when the original guard was `NONE`.

### Credited-world reconciliation: missing design text for Root

**Implementation is stopped for Root under TASK continuation 08 section 3.** The
ordinary scheduler and `RocketEscrowAudit.attempt` are unchanged and cannot return
`proved=true`. No RAM absence authorizes retirement, marker removal or release.

W11a section 4 specifies the prefix/proof/L2 ordering, but does not freeze the
native rocket mutation envelope. The concrete adapters expose two different
signatures: existing propulsion-operation endpoints use
`RocketFuelState.resourceSignature()`, whereas the settlement observer/disk reader
use `hash("arce-escrow-rocket-1:" + logical + ":" + snapshotHash + ":" + fuelSignature)`.
`EscrowPrefix.classify` compares the endpoint signatures literally. Reusing the
settlement observer as an ordinary driver would therefore reject a normal native
operation as OTHER_STATE. Silently changing existing operation hashes or using
ordinary `updateFlightData` to bypass a pending-resource guard is not an acceptable
implementation choice.

There is also an unresolved reconstruction boundary. W27a section 8 freezes the
bay dispatch/interlock hooks, not a credited-source reconstruction envelope.
Section 4.1 explicitly says that T lacks the source escrow wrapper, refuses a
non-neutral source, and names W27f complete-envelope recovery as not designed
there. W11b2's existing transferred-entity rule instead creates a new epoch-0
physical identity. These facts do not define how to rebuild a missing credited
physical source without inventing or resetting its ledger/epoch/resources.

The following is the missing proposed contract text for Root to freeze, **not an
accepted schema change or implemented recovery guarantee**:

1. The first ordinary adapter supports an already-loaded stationary native rocket
   and a registered supported source only. Until a production source exists, only
   the explicitly gated fixture supplies it. Before any debit, validate the entire
   connected component, checked journal guards, owners, exact logical/physical
   identities, Level/type/origin, empty occupancy and both endpoint prefixes.
   Unsupported, relocated, duplicate or loaded-missing identities stay in manual
   repair; unloaded ends wait without tickets. Missing identities are not rebuilt
   from a historical snapshot and never release resources through RAM absence.
2. Existing normal propulsion records retain their fuel-vector signatures; the
   immutable complete live envelope is additionally frozen for proof validation.
   A private, single-action apply authority binds the acknowledged ledger/member
   digests, journal guard, endpoint reference, owner, snapshot, position, flight
   state, old fuel/section and exact computed new fuel/section. It authorizes only
   the next recorded per-endpoint prefix step. It neither makes `idle()` globally
   true nor admits an ordinary public resource mutation or live NBT replacement.
   Fuel and its section/marker are published atomically, with no historical refund.
3. Apply missing prefix steps in ascending endpoint order, within the existing
   visit/work budgets. Freeze each complete frontier; acquire jointly live own-store,
   original-worker force and stable-readback proofs for every affected slot. Retain
   an exact checked journal-authority lease through L2 capture and replacement.
   A superseding save, changed identity/guard, veto, failed or UNKNOWN write keeps
   authority and locks. Only exact L2 acknowledgement retires a contiguous prefix
   and its required floors; pruning never lowers epochs or changes resources.
   Orphan handling needs current-ledger/watermark and identity/receipt validation,
   not a retired marker as proof of a former physical identity.
4. Supporting missing credited-source reconstruction is a separate extension to
   that boundary. Before implementing it, freeze the durable complete-envelope
   fields, independent schema, byte bound, old-record migration/refusal policy and
   source-versus-new-destination epoch rule. A hash or the old T snapshots alone
   cannot supply the missing wrapper. Bay implementation remains outside this TASK.
5. Native acceptance must cut before/after every newly implemented binding, L1,
   endpoint-proof, L2 replacement/callback and pruning acknowledgement, with two
   restarts and source/JAR pins. Include BEFORE/AFTER and chained prefixes, changed
   guards, missing/duplicate/orphan identities, superseded proofs, veto and UNKNOWN.
   Existing O2-O4 settlement cuts do not count as ordinary-reconciliation evidence.

Root must decide whether the loaded-only/manual-missing boundary above is the
intended credited-world slice, or supply the complete-envelope reconstruction
contract before implementation resumes. No normal reconciliation code or native
reconciliation guarantee is delivered by this continuation. Signed per-fluid loss
accounting remains open independently of that decision.

## Continuation 09

### Root decision required: native prefix identity across fuel banks

**Protocol implementation is stopped for the TASK's proof-change decision.** At
`a135d140ac519538344fcd8f607a6b21d3f6923f`, two new assertions reproduce a conflict
between the existing bank-keyed prefix implementation and design 2 section 5.1's
whole-native-vector frontier. No acknowledgement order, hold, replay exclusion,
production admission or runtime proof is changed in this continuation.

The exact conflict is:

- `EscrowEndpoint.java:23-25` includes `Unit` in endpoint-key equality.
  `EscrowPrefix.java:28-45` groups and validates links using that key.
- `RocketEscrowSection.java:11-25` has one epoch and one contiguous marker suffix
  for the physical rocket, not independent PRIMARY and OXIDIZER epochs.
- `RocketPropulsionFuel.java:27-30` signs both banks and their shared revision;
  design 2 section 5.1 requires that unchanged `FUEL_VECTOR_V1` projection.
- Sequential PRIMARY then OXIDIZER fills produce a valid shared epoch-2 wrapper
  with both markers. The ledger accepts the two records, but classification of
  the PRIMARY chain returns `OTHER`, not `AFTER`.
- Sequential PRIMARY, OXIDIZER, PRIMARY fills produce contiguous native vector
  transitions at epochs 0, 1, 2 and 3. Ledger construction rejects them with
  `Noncontiguous endpoint chain`: its PRIMARY-only chain skips epoch 1 to 2.

These are pure-model reproductions using actual `RocketFuelState` native
bipropellant vectors, canonical native Fluid payloads and codec-round-tripped
rocket wrappers. Three distinct registered fixture-source references satisfy
the one-operation-per-device rule. They are not live endpoint/store proofs,
production-loader tests or S2 evidence. Single OXIDIZER and two PRIMARY fills
pass as controls. The native descriptor explicitly permits registered vanilla
water in both roles; no unregistered Fluid or unavailable mod registry is needed.

**Requested Root clarification, not applied:** confirm whether section 5.1's
per-endpoint proof identity is the physical rocket shared by both banks, with
`Unit` retained as the operation's exact bank selector rather than a separate
prefix identity. Otherwise specify how bank-keyed chains prove the shared
epoch/vector/marker frontier. Changing this grouping changes the inputs to
classification, frozen frontiers, partial retirement and settlement observation
deduplication; it is not safe to silently change only the apply loop. The frozen
note does not explicitly resolve this key equality. The TASK says a proof change
needs Root first, so the worker has not coded around it or inferred approval.

The new failing assertions remain enabled. The existing implementation holds or
refuses these cases; this run demonstrates missing required mixed-bank progress,
not an observed duplicate credit or unsafe automatic overwrite.

### Step 3 authority/writer checkpoint

This is partial implementation, not completion of step 3 or continuation 10.
`EscrowFlightAuthority` supplies bounded immutable arm, terminal-outcome and
exact terminal-prune transitions. It issues serials from the root watermark,
preserves that watermark through cancellation/pruning, checks protected transfer
references and current debit history, and advances receipt-backed heads without
editing receipts. Ordinary flights create no head. This implementation chooses
checked F8 cleanup before another F1; optional COMMITTED-predecessor replacement
is not implemented or inferred from a named journal guard.

`EscrowFlightWrites` coalesces independent logical-rocket requests into one
immutable generation. Queue plus in-flight requests are bounded to 64, and each
logical rocket has at most one. Each change receives the actual writer
generation, cannot alter operations/floors/receipts or another logical rocket,
and rechecks retained atomic proof/journal leases before replacement. UNKNOWN
retains the candidate, requests and locks without a success callback. Existing
10-tick writer cadence is unchanged. The coordinator gives pending flight work
precedence over idle autosaves but still permits pending reason transitions to
persist, avoiding mutual starvation. Existing generic recovery/transaction
policy refuses ARMED and terminal entries rather than consuming them as legacy
authority; unrelated logical rockets retain their existing behavior.

These APIs remain package-private and have no production or native launch
caller. Synthetic A0 hash/lease fixtures are not native checkpoint proof.
There is no F1 own-store/force/readback adapter, governed record-3 dispatcher,
action permit or operational handoff in this checkpoint. The model's finish and
prune candidates are not permission to skip those prerequisites. No on-disk
field, schema, size ceiling, legacy checksum or admission policy changes.

The final isolated runs of `gradlew.bat --offline test --tests
'*EscrowFlightAuthorityTest' --console=plain` and the equivalent
`'*EscrowFlightWritesTest'` command pass 7 and 4 tests respectively, with no
failures/errors/skips. Tests cover serials, cancellation, receipt immutability,
successor refuelling at the model level, stale authority, protected transfer
references, source wrapper refusal, 64/65-entry and debit-history bounds,
overflow, generic recovery refusal, eight-request coalescing, replacement-time
proof invalidation, UNKNOWN resolution, scoped changes and checked reload.
The eight-request case is not an eight-flight benchmark or a docs/17 pass.

Development runs are retained under
`D:/GitHub/ARCE-Task-Evidence/root-queue/W11b2-C10/authority-01/`.
The first writer run has 8 tests and 2 failures: its fixture used `escrow.dat`
instead of the managed type's stable filename, so it never reached the injected

H1 is commit `1b775d57`. These are separate logical commits on the assigned local
branch; nothing was pushed or merged. No acknowledgement order, proof, replay
exclusion, persistence byte/node/depth bound or decode cardinality was changed.
L2's previously accepted receipt dictionary and its inspector were not changed.

The measured admission maximum is **5**, while `EscrowLimits.PER_ROCKET` remains
**8** for saved-section/intent/root decoding. `EscrowSettlementCapacityTest` encodes
and decodes roots with one through eight pending fills for both fixture and native
endpoints. Settlement reservation passes at five and refuses at six through eight.
For zero through four retired markers plus one live native fill, the actual encoded
receipts are respectively 1,700, 1,773, 1,846, 1,919 and 1,992 bytes, using the
worst permitted modified-UTF actor-name encoding. Five retired markers plus a new
fill is refused before queue/sequence mutation. This is an upper admission count,
not a promise that every payload or connected component fits: the unchanged byte
budgets can refuse earlier. The existing maximum-fluid-payload rejection remains.

The combined-reservation regression fills unrelated offline components and floors
up to the actual boundary. Its last fitting root contains 29 floors. A subsequent
floor O1 passes the old floor-only sizing check but fails the combined check before
writer capture, without changing the ledger. Fixed reservations for unrelated
components remain deliberately conservative rather than charging zero bytes.

M4's pre-start regression uses a decodable but non-idempotent stored payload and
checks two refusals, unchanged raw bytes and no migration backup. The pre-start
adapter logs one bounded ERROR line (under 512 characters), without attaching an
exception stack to that log call, and refuses startup. A late baseline failure logs
one bounded WARN line (under 256 characters); repeated binds/ticks do not repeat
the log, throw or write. Its scope is escrow-dependent actions for this server
session, until a stopped-server whole-world repair and restart. Terrain, entities,
POI, journals and player data must remain a consistent set; restoring/deleting only
the escrow file is not recovery. Writer unavailability after transient UNKNOWN
outcomes is a separate condition: the existing exact-candidate retry keeps running
and its resolution assertions still pass. Root owns the corresponding risk/ADR
wording; this continuation did not edit those files or add a save-veto mechanism.

Files changed by area are the escrow model/coordinator/writer/capacity/pruning
implementation and test probes, the pre-start migration adapter, ten Java unit

No assets, upstream files, data generation, registration, build scripts, workflows,

## Continuation 12

### F1/F6 proof boundary requiring Root clarification

Implementation is stopped before changing flight authority. Design 2 section 5.4
F1 allows cancellation after ARMED replacement for the exact unchanged source E
and checked no-T state. The cancellation paragraph below F8 instead says that an
exact-source return follows F6-F8 with an absent destination proved. Section 1's
cancellation row likewise requires source plus destination absence. It is not
explicit whether the first rule is an exception to the latter two for a stop
before F2 has written any PREPARED record. This is a proof-scope question, not a
request to relax an IO failure or invent a successful acknowledgement.

The existing durable representation makes the distinction material:

- `EscrowFlightTables.Armed` stores the destination UUID and envelope digest, but
  no destination Level, chunk, position or snapshot. Its source binding is also a
  digest, not a recorded-slot reference.
- F1 checkpoints the unchanged source E before COUNTDOWN. A first-flight source
  need not contain the proposed plan or destination. Full destination envelopes
  belong to the governed journal, which F2 writes only after ARMED.
- Consequently, the ARMED-replaced/no-F2 state has no recorded destination slot
  for an own-store/force/readback absence proof. A digest is not an invertible
  location. Searching arbitrary Levels/chunks or reconstructing a destination
  from current pad selection would not prove the frozen destination.

Root must specify whether pre-F2 cancellation uses exact source E plus a fresh
checked no-T lease **without** a destination-slot proof, or requires an approved
bounded durable slot reference and its proof. The former may be the intended
specific meaning of F1; this record does not claim it is already an approved
exception to F6. Persisting the journal before ARMED, silently adding an authority
field, treating UNKNOWN as no-T, and leaving every valid early-stop flight in
permanent REPAIR are not implemented alternatives. TASK-W11B2-CONTINUE-12 requires
stopping for a proof/acknowledgement/hold change needing Root.

targeted this worktree. The write scope is the W11b2 escrow/rocket code and its
tests plus this record; this stopped continuation changes only an A0 test and
this record. There are no production, schema, bound, reservation, asset,
data-generation, ADR, risk, status, ledger, build or workflow edits. No subagent,
push, shared-branch merge or unrelated worktree edit is performed.

`EscrowArmedRestartBoundaryTest` exercises the actual checked writers and codecs
at ARMED replacement before callback, and after callback but before F2. Each case
performs two independent ledger baseline reads and checked empty-journal reloads.
It checks the retained ARMED/serial/digests, absent PREPARED record, no automatic
acknowledgement from decode, and rejection by generic recovery, active pruning
and re-arming. Synthetic envelope hashes and a model proof supplier deliberately
do not claim a native F1 checkpoint, F6 absence proof, server restart or S2 cut.
Execution results and the delivery commit are recorded below after verification.

The isolated `gradlew.bat --offline test --tests '*EscrowArmedRestartBoundaryTest'
--console=plain` run passes both new tests. No runtime failure or native
recoverability claim is inferred from that model-level result. The test-source
commit precedes the required whole-suite/GameTest verification.

### Four failures: comparison and demonstrated cause

The original four classes passed at entry `9b9b6ed0`. Every requested revision
was then exported to one owned Temp directory and tested independently:
`e03fa37f`, `cbde4d21`, `db9e884f`, `ba66a268`, `f953ca4e`; each passed all 24
tests. This exhaustive targeted comparison found **no reproducible good/bad
pair**, so it does not identify a first-bad C12 commit or claim a successful
`git bisect`. The writer, coordinator, checked file/policy and four test classes
are byte-unchanged between those endpoints.

The demonstrated defect is the tests' clock coupling: each five-second polling
loop advances one owner tick per sleep, but unavailable needs three completed
UNKNOWN attempts and two 200-owner-tick retry backoffs. In a controlled export
of `f953ca4e`, replacing only those tests' 2/5 ms sleeps with 16 ms sleeps, while
keeping deadlines, assertions and production code unchanged, reproduced exactly
the four reported failures. The loops stopped at owner ticks 301, 305, 307 and
304, before the third completion. This establishes the scheduling dependency,
not the original failed C12 JVM's exact operating-system scheduling trigger.

The fix awaits real IO completion before advancing the test owner clock instead
of extending a timeout. The new boundary test pins UNKNOWN completions at ticks
1/202/403, the two full backoffs, identical retry digest, unavailable only on the
third completion, and subsequent successful recovery. The settlement test also
keeps ticks monotonic during recovery rather than restarting them at 211.
`DIAGNOSIS.md`, the revision comparison script, controlled-failure XML and the
empty driven-path diff retain this evidence.

## Continuation 15

### Root decision required: capacity checks versus persistence of sticky repair reasons

The all-root-growing-write requirement cannot be extended literally to the
ordinary transition/autosave snapshot without changing its refusal behavior.
`EscrowReservationTransitionTest.admittedOperationsCanPersistTheirStickyRepairReasons`
reproduces this using 64 independent existing fixture operations. Every admission
passes `requireAdmission` and `preflight(candidate, operationId)`, and the
coordinator acknowledges all 64 through its real L1 writer. Changing all
64 reasons from `NONE` to `RELOCATED_ENDPOINT` grows the encoded root from
129,938 to 130,706 bytes, but `preflight(repaired)` rejects it with
`Settlement/flight root reservation exhausted`. The existing checked autosave
successfully persists all 64 sticky reasons, verified against its durable file.

The cause is the reservation rule, not the actual root-byte limit: admission
previews the newly merged component, while later ineligible components are
charged the fixed 12 KiB operation plus 2 KiB receipt reservation, together
with both 64-entry flight-table reservations. Those fixed amounts total
64 * (12 + 2 + 1 + 1) KiB = 1 MiB before root framing. Adding the check to
`EscrowCoordinator.snapshot(false, ...)` would prevent this repair-state save
(and currently throw from its capture). This session does not add that check,
clear a reason, weaken a bound, or add a save-refusal exception.

Root needs to decide how to cover this transition: for example, reserve its
fixed worst case before L1, or explicitly define a non-authority-growing repair
save policy. Existing decisions allow acknowledged-root reservation comparison
for O3/O4, not this ordinary sticky-reason autosave. The choice affects admission
capacity and/or save refusal and is not made here. Implementation stops at the
checked dispatcher/source-action prerequisite; step 3 is still incomplete.

### Completed prerequisite and exact unfinished boundary

`EscrowGovernedDispatch` checks the acknowledged journal object/phase lease,
the exact ARMED tuple and generation, root-wide retired-T/floor exclusions,
and any READY anchor/frontier/serial. It distinguishes active from terminal
cleanup-only authority. Views are invalidated by replaced roots/journal phases,
unavailability, endpoint invalidation and runtime shutdown. Bay-first fence
classification consults this dispatcher without an entity lookup or ticket.
It still refuses generic record-3 recovery and callbacks; a positive route is
not an action or storage-proof permit.

The package-private source-permit issuer provides `START_COUNTDOWN`,
`COMPLETE_COUNTDOWN` and `BEGIN_TRANSIT`. Each consumes a one-use checked
authority plus the executor's final boundary, compares the complete source
envelope and preserves fuel, debit IDs, snapshot and wrapper. Timers and the
exact top-of-ascent position are checked. The entity adapter accepts only this
permit and requires its issuance tick. These are unit-tested prerequisites;
the live executor does not issue them yet. No governed flight is enabled by
this checkpoint, and the existing ordinary idle/floor rules are not relaxed.

### Step 3 implementation boundary, recorded before editing

The governed source execution must use the checked record-3 dispatcher, not
the legacy `KEEP_SOURCE` election or an ordinary idle exemption. Its complete
source observation and final action permit must retain the checkpointed
resources and wrapper, validate allowed plan progression and reject stale
callbacks, changed identities and duplicates before any effect. A route alone
does not grant ticket, publication, removal, passenger or cleanup authority.
The original acknowledgement order, proofs, replay exclusions and all bounds
remain unchanged. Tests and the exact delivered boundary are recorded below.

### Exact-source action boundary

Added the checked, action-specific `EscrowGovernedSourceRecovery` permit and
its `EscrowRecoveryFence.reason` overload. `RESUME_EXACT_SOURCE` retains the
present complete E or permitted COUNTDOWN/ASCENT/TRANSIT progression without
replacing any field. `RETURN_EXACT_SOURCE` transforms that validated present
flight and its position only; it does not use legacy `stationarySource`,
reconstruct a missing source, refund fuel, change the wrapper or snapshot,
write a terminal outcome, prune the journal, or make ordinary idle true.
The entity adapter consumes the one-use permit in its issuing server tick.
It repeats the complete source, loaded census, checked root/record and final
executor boundary. Duplicate, missing, changed or unloaded observations refuse.
The 64-candidate truncation boundary refuses, and observations copy only a
unique source, not every ambiguous candidate's full snapshot. Existing NBT,
count, schema and reservation bounds are unchanged.

This permit covers only PREPARED source authority. A destination-authoritative
return still requires the separate publication/absence proof; empty RAM is not
that proof. Physical occupant changes still need their own passenger action.
Legacy recovery retains every old fence, including `KEEP_SOURCE`'s credited
resource refusal. Source resume does not yet rebase a restarted phase clock.

The first isolated test exposed an existing deferred-authority defect:
`acknowledgedLease()` checks only the mutation sequence, so a dirty journal
without a sequence change could leave a cached dispatcher authority valid.
Final owner-thread validation now additionally requires a current acknowledged,
non-stopping, non-failed journal and its exact record. The off-thread IO lease
is unchanged. Stop/abort, dirty state, superseded roots and terminal outcomes
all invalidate the deferred permit. This enforces the existing design's
checked-current prerequisite; it changes no acknowledgement order or proof.

Changed areas are the governed source action/fence, entity adapter, dispatcher
and journal availability predicate, plus one unit class and one GameTest class.
The GameTest uses a detached acknowledged authority fixture and a real native
entity to exercise final-boundary refusal, resume and return. It is explicitly
not F1 checkpoint, live-dispatch, F6 or complete-flight evidence. The existing
large entity and journal classes remain below 800 lines and gain only thin
adapter/read-only methods; domain validation remains in the separate class.

### F4 publication sub-step

The dispatcher now acknowledges the fixed destination UUID at F4 before
initialization or publication. Fresh own-store, original-worker force and
stable-readback proofs cover both recorded entity slots. A shared slot has one
capture that proves complete source E and destination absence together. The
one-use initializer installs the frozen destination envelope, its already
computed debit and its independent ledger-bound epoch 0. It never debits,
rewrites, removes or unlocks the source. Loaded census, bay/transaction checks,
landing availability, checked authority and proof validity are rechecked at
the action boundary. No destination UUID is regenerated on retry.

F4 recovery accepts the exact checkpointed source E or a permitted F3
progression. This follows the frozen F1/F4 mixed-save-order contract: an F4
acknowledgement can survive while disk still has F1's E. It does not rerun a
source debit, infer a launch, reconstruct a source or change acknowledgement,
proof, hold or replay rules. Source validation uses continuation 24's logical
phase clock, not a direct comparison with rolled-back Level time.

The new live checks extend the existing normal and clock fixtures after all
their original F3 assertions. They exercise publication/republication, unchanged
source credit, a shared entity slot, F4 recovery with only F1's source and
continued resource locks. The source-only native clock fixture now persists a
blocked destination before its pre-F1 baseline, keeping its original

### Step 1 implementation boundary

The frozen section 5.5 rule requires ticket activation after checked ACTIVE
classification, before waiting for loaded ends. The previous dispatcher
added tickets only after an executor returned a non-HELD result; each
executor first required both ends to be loaded, so an unloaded end could
never become ready through this dispatcher.

The dispatcher now refreshes the existing two recorded-origin tickets
after the bay-first and checked ACTIVE classification, then waits before
entity lookup or any phase effect. The original complete loaded-end/action
boundary is unchanged and still runs before each effect and callback.
Ticket type, level, 400-tick expiry, two-origin bound and eight-record visit
budget are unchanged. Refreshing does not call a synchronous chunk getter.
HOLD/CLEANUP classification, ordinary reconciliation, pruning and held
repair do not enter this activation helper. An unavailable Level remains
an unchanged wait; it does not create a Level or redirect its coordinates.
There is no acknowledgement, proof, schema or replay-policy change.

The new A1 fixtures use native source store/force/readback, then explicitly
serialized checked startup roots. The source-restart case lets the native
source unload before seeding a checked ARMED fixture and loading a fresh
journal object, then allows ordinary dispatch to reload it. The existing
ledger coordinator remains installed for shared-world lifecycle safety.
This is an in-process startup/reload
fixture, not a claimed native process-restart cut. These shared-root reload
fixtures have isolated batches. Separate tests cover an unloaded destination
and a permanently unavailable recorded Level. No log expectation is added.
